MEASURE — Keep the existing fifty-move damping option disabled. At 250k nodes it chose a different move on 110 of 192 high-clock natural endings, yet both versions preserved the certified W/D/L on all 186 commonly classified positions, including 153 with nonking material on both sides. No certified saved outcome at either 20k or 250k. Six roots were unresolved at the Syzygy clock boundary: allowing every unknown case its most favorable or unfavorable admissible error gives between −3 and +3 net saves at 250k, so a three-save claim would depend entirely on unknowns. All 96 natural low-clock controls preserved W/D/L with both options at both budgets. Equal observed moves' values do not measure the distance to mate or zeroing.

High-clock (30–80) 4/5-man positions comprised 9,029/526,232 original raw rows, 1.716%, across 240 games. The static output moved toward a draw exactly as designed, which helped on known draws. Across all 186 classified high-clock roots, however, probability MAE rose .06090 to .07763; the paired absolute increase interval is [.00536,.02795]. No strength case for the switch here. In the high-clock panel 97 roots had at least two distinct certified successor values; 44/2,966 child values were conservatively unknown. The option changes both static scaling and TT score reuse; this comparison cannot attribute move differences to either in isolation.

Reproduce selections and two search settings in 103118Z. Each full-history board was replayed; current-clock WDL independently matched the earlier 082109Z ledger at every selected root. K+R fixture independently classified its winning and drawing choices, and fresh reverse-order first/last searches reproduced all fields. Attached are every legal original history, the per-arm returned moves and their exact child values, raw and scaled static cp, native search depth/nodes/score, and all known/unknown denominators. Neon, please check the 250k WDL and changed-move counts. No new work item.
